The correct approach:

1. For each possible non-decreasing subsequence, we add the product of its elements to the total
2. We use dynamic programming to avoid recomputing the same subsequences

After further analysis, here's the solution:

```python
def solve():
    MOD = 1000000007
    
    n = int(input())
    a = list(map(int, input().split()))
    
    # We'll use DP where dp[j] represents the sum of products 
    # of all distinct non-decreasing subsequences ending with value j
    # But we need to be careful about duplicates
    
    # Instead, let's use a different approach:
    # For each position, we can extend existing subsequences
    # We maintain a dictionary or array for the current sum of products
    
    # Keep track of current sum of products for subsequences
    # We'll use a dictionary to store the sum of products for subsequences ending with each value
    
    # Initially, we have subsequences of single elements
    # For each new element, we can create new subsequences by taking it alone
    # or by appending it to existing subsequences ending with values <= current value
    
    # dp[val] = sum of products of all possible subsequences ending with value 'val'
    dp = {}
    
    # Process each element
    for val in a:
        new_dp = dp.copy()
        
        # Add the new single element subsequence
        # This contributes val to the total
        new_dp[val] = (new_dp.get(val, 0) + val) % MOD
        
        # For each existing subsequence ending with value v where v <= val
        # we can extend it by adding val, which gives a new subsequence with product = old_product * val
        for v in list(dp.keys()):
            if v <= val:
                old_prod = dp[v]
                new_product = (old_prod * val) % MOD
                new_dp[val] = (new_dp[val] + new_product) % MOD
        
        dp = new_dp
    
    # Sum all values in dp (sum of products of all subsequences)
    result = 0
    for val in dp:
        result = (result + dp[val]) % MOD
    
    print(result)

solve()
```

Here's the correct approach with proper DP:

```python
MOD = 1000000007

n = int(input())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))

# dp[i] will store the sum of products of all distinct non-decreasing subsequences 
# that end with value i
dp = {}

for val in a:
    # Create a new state that includes the current value
    new_dp = dp.copy()
    
    # Add the new single element subsequence
    new_dp[val] = (new_dp.get(val, 0) + val) % MOD
    
    # Extend all existing subsequences ending with value <= val
    for v in list(dp.keys()):
        if v <= val:
            # Extend subsequence ending with v by adding val
            # The product becomes (product of original) * val
            old_product = dp[v]
            new_product = (old_product * val) % MOD
            new_dp[val] = (new_dp[val] + new_product) % MOD
    
    dp = new_dp

# Sum all values in dp
result = 0
for val in dp:
    result = (result + dp[val]) % MOD

print(result)
```

```python
MOD = 1000000007

n = int(input())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))

# dp[i] = sum of products of all distinct non-decreasing subsequences that end with value i
dp = {}

for val in a:
    new_dp = dp.copy()
    
    # Add new subsequence of length 1 with value val
    new_dp[val] = (new_dp.get(val, 0) + val) % MOD
    
    # Extend existing subsequences ending with value <= val
    for v in dp.keys():
        if v <= val:
            old_sum = dp[v]
            new_product = (old_sum * val) % MOD
            new_dp[val] = (new_dp[val] + new_product) % MOD
    
    dp = new_dp

result = sum(dp.values()) % MOD
print(result)
```

```python
MOD = 1000000007

n = int(input())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))

# dp[val] = sum of products of all distinct non-decreasing subsequences ending with val
dp = {}

    new_dp = dp.copy()

    new_dp[val] = (new_dp.get(val, 0) + val) % MOD

    for v in dp.keys():

            # The product of the new subsequence would be (product of old) * val
            old_sum = dp[v]
            new_product = (old_sum * val) % MOD
            new_dp[val] = (new_dp[val] + new_product) % MOD
    
    dp = new_dp
```
